What this evaluates

Does contract-term extraction hold up on long, scanned document packets whose amendments have to be sorted out? 60 synthetic packets of 7,000–20,000 words (an agreement with the graded articles in its back half, five schedules, 4–16 SOWs with their own terms, caps and laws as distractors) at three scan-noise levels; 44 carry a trap: amendment chains filed out of order, a restatement that supersedes an earlier amendment, unsigned, one-party and future-effective amendments, renewal and term changes, and clauses that are simply absent. Extract eight terms as they stand on the status date.

A pass of terms-check means: All eight extracted fields match the key the generator computed (binding amendments applied in effective-date order, a restatement superseding what came before it, absent clauses null).

directional

Recommend deepseek-v4.1-flash · lowest cost

$0.0054 per success · required check 57/60 passed

Of the 6 candidates that pass every gate, deepseek-v4.1-flash has the lowest generation cost per success ($0.0054). It beats the control sonnet-5.

No pairwise comparison was available this run.

Directional only: the spec declares no minimum meaningful difference. Also deepseek-v4.1-flash: required-check-pass-rate 57/60 has a 95% interval down to 0.863, below the gate 0.9; deepseek-v4.1-flash: reliability 57/60 has a 95% interval down to 0.863, below the gate 0.9.

Selection each eligibility gate removes candidates, the operating mode picks among the rest · rule select-v4

  1. required-check pass rate ≥ 90.0% — removed: glm-5.3 (required check passed 88.3% (53/60)); qwen3.8-max (required check passed 86.7% (52/60)). Remaining: sonnet-5, kimi-k3, deepseek-v4.1-flash, opus-5.5, gpt-5.6-sol, deepseek-v4-pro.
  2. reliability ≥ 90.0% — removed: nobody removed. Remaining: sonnet-5, kimi-k3, deepseek-v4.1-flash, opus-5.5, gpt-5.6-sol, deepseek-v4-pro.

Passed every gate: sonnet-5, kimi-k3, deepseek-v4.1-flash, opus-5.5, gpt-5.6-sol, deepseek-v4-pro. The judge's win rate is for reference only and takes no part in selection.

Notes on the extraction: the Master Services Agreement fixes the initial term (24 months), automatic renewal (12-month successive terms), and non-renewal notice (120 days). The MSA contains no governing-law clause (only a Delaware venue provision, Section 25), and each SOW names a different state's law, so no single governing law is stated for the agreement. The MSA also contains no general liability cap — Section 23 only excludes consequential damages; the per-SOW caps (e.g., $400,000) are engagement-specific, not a general cap on a party's total liability under the agreement.

Fields this run did not observe GAPS.md

  • ttft_ms: non-streaming calls; no time to first token.
  • cache: the provider reported cached prompt tokens on some calls; recorded under tokens.cached, no cache cost breakdown.
  • deployment_ref.region / tier: route plus who served the call (OpenRouter's upstream provider, or the model id a direct API answered with); region and service tier are not exposed.
  • human_intervention: not observed; no trace format read here says whether the agent waited on a person.
  • tool_calls, turns: not observed; no candidate declared cli.trace, so what the agents did between input and output is not recorded.
  • checkpoint: not observed; no trace format read here says whether the agent resumed from one.
  • transport retry: model-API calls retry transient failures (HTTP 408/409/425/429, 5xx, network) twice with exponential backoff and no jitter; per-trial retry counts are not in scores.jsonl, only retry cost.
  • evaluator errors: none in this run.
  • pairwise judging: **not run** — the spec declares methods without pairwise-swap. No preference direction is observed in this run.
  • effective N: 11 of 60 inputs separated any two candidates on task success; 49 every candidate passed, 0 gave everyone the same partial rate. Only the separating inputs carry evidence for a choice (items.json).
  • pairwise judging uses only the first successful output per (candidate, input); repeated trials feed absolute scores only. The selector reads the pairwise score only to rank judge-preference, and to break a tie on assurance in highest-assurance.
  • statistical uncertainty: the chosen candidate's gated rates carry Wilson 95% intervals, and a highest-assurance pick must beat the runner-up on an exact sign test over inputs (recommendation.json evidence). Inputs generated from one template are still treated as independent, which overstates certainty when they share a template.
